HOUSING, WATER AND BASIC SANITATION SECTORS

Sectoral measures – Ministry of Housing, City and Territory

INTEGRATED SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT (GIRS). — This measure is framed in Decree 0670 of 2025 – Zero Waste Program, the Sectoral Comprehensive Climate Change Management Plan (PIGCCS) of the housing sector, the Green Growth Policy and the national guidelines for Integrated Municipal Solid Waste Management. Its purpose is to promote the design, implementation and operation of solid waste treatment and utilization technologies as a complementary activity.

The main actions covered by this measure are:

  • Management and promotion of Mechanical Biological Treatment (MBT) Systems: drive the implementation of technologies that allow the separation, treatment and valorization of organic and inorganic waste, favoring the reduction of the volume disposed in sanitary landfills.
  • Utilization of recyclable materials: strengthen source separation and recycling processes, integrated into the Zero Waste Decree, in order to reduce GHG emissions, save energy and raw materials, and consolidate the circular economy under criteria of economic viability and environmental sustainability.
  • Capture, conduction and burning of biogas in sanitary landfills: promote the installation of biogas control and management systems, in coordination with the PIGCCS Housing and the Zero Waste Decree, with the objective of reducing greenhouse gas emissions, diversifying renewable energy sources and generating environmental and economic benefits.
  • Biogas utilization: promote the design and implementation of biogas use and utilization systems (electrical or thermal co-generation, and biomethane production) captured in existing sanitary landfills and new cells, contributing to the energy transition and climate change mitigation.

In accordance with the Peace and Climate Action Methodological Matrix NDC 3.0, which integrates the five strategic Peace lines based on international metrics and aligned with the approaches of Just Transition, SDGs, Human Rights, Gender and Children, Alternative 1 – Implementation Enabling Term is developed for this measure, linked mainly to Peace lines 1. Governance and Transparency, 3. Human Security and Community Resilience, 4. Sustainable Development and Positive Peace. Based on these lines, the measure aligns with:

  • Just Transition: Contributes to element (a) of the JTWP by strengthening enabling conditions for the reconversion of the waste system toward circular models, and to element (e) by promoting economic sustainability for recyclers and managers within the transition toward cleaner technologies.
  • Global SDGs: Aligned with SDGs 11.6.1, 12.5.1 and 13.2.2, promoting waste reduction, improvement in urban environmental quality and climate coherence in territorial planning.
  • Human Rights: Contributes to the right to a dignified life, health and a healthy environment, by preventing contamination of soils, air and water. It also contributes to inclusion, non-discrimination and decent working conditions for recyclers and vulnerable communities, under the principle of do no harm.
  • Gender: Promotes progress toward gender equality in accordance with the guide for the inclusion of the gender approach for women in the planning and budgeting cycle of the housing, city and territory sector, taking into account the approaches set out in Resolution 0467 of 22 JUL 2025, analyzing how the diverse conditions and characteristics of people and groups (social, economic, gender, age, ethno-racial, disability, gender identity, sexual orientation) are considered in the implementation of this measure.
  • Children: The measure is articulated with the following MISNI indicators: DI5, DI10, DI13, JE6 and JE9. By increasing walking and cycling through safe infrastructure and inclusive territorial planning, it reduces congestion, emissions and road accidents, improving air quality, health and safe access for girls, boys and adolescents to essential services.

Analytical basis: GIRS is a strategic measure for the transition toward a circular and low-carbon economy, reducing methane emissions in sanitary landfills and strengthening governance of the public cleaning service under criteria of transparency and sustainability. By integrating MBT technologies, recycling and biogas utilization, it generates benefits in public health, air quality, green employment and community resilience in urban and rural areas. It also improves equity and social cohesion by including informal recyclers in formal management circuits, promotes shared environmental responsibility and contributes to positive peace through the generation of decent employment and sustainable development in territories.

ENERGY UTILIZATION OF BIOGAS IN WASTEWATER TREATMENT

The central objective is to promote the adoption of biogas valorization technologies — such as electrical or thermal co-generation, and biomethane production — in plants that already have anaerobic infrastructure, promoting criteria of efficiency, sustainability and regulatory compliance. These actions will contribute to strengthening the circular economy of the sanitation sector and improving its climate performance without increasing net emissions from the expansion of treatment coverage.

The measure incorporates the experience of national biogas utilization projects in large-scale plants as a technical reference, seeking to replicate and expand these solutions to other treatment systems in the country. Likewise, the definition of specific targets for biogas capture, burning and energy use is planned once the national mitigation model is updated and detailed information on the technological and operational capacities of the WWTPs at the national level is available, with participation of the relevant actors.

2030

2035

2030

Capture, burning and biogas utilization.

Promote systems for the capture, conduction and energy utilization of biogas in the main WWTPs in the country.

Mitigation potential:

No information is available to determine a baseline and biogas use and utilization targets with the proposed horizons. Therefore, from the VASB it is proposed that the target be indicative referring to the following elements:

  • Sectoral regulations do not specifically include biogas management and by-products of wastewater treatment in the forms filled in by sewerage service providers through the SUI (Single Information System) administered by the Superintendency of Public Household Services. In this sense, there is no quality and timely information at the sub-sectoral level that allows establishing assumptions to know the GHG emission reduction potentials and have quality information for decision-making.
  • The actions are aimed at advancing in increasing sewerage service coverage that allows conducting wastewater to WWTPs (economies of scale). Additionally, municipalities that already have some type of treatment are oriented toward compliance with the environmental regulatory framework, especially regarding organic load removal framed in the discharge permit and payment of the retributive rate, which leads to focusing their activities on the main treatment components.
  • The projects that generate the greatest GHG reductions are associated with treatment in WWTPs of large municipalities, however, there are no financing sources for the development of the infrastructure.
  • The technical and regulatory instruments for the injection of biogas and electrical energy into the national networks are limited.
  • In the value chain of public household service provision, potable water needs are addressed first, then the conduction of wastewater and finally its treatment. Additionally, in not all municipalities is it possible to develop projects associated with biogas use and utilization due to climatic conditions or the amount of flow treated. Compliance milestones:
  • Conduct technical and financial analyses to define minimum requirements for biogas capture, burning and utilization processes in the country, so that guidelines can be generated for decision-making.
  • Submit a request to the Superintendency of Public Household Services for the creation of an information module in the SUI (Single Information System) to obtain information on biogas and other by-products of treatment that is reported by operators of Wastewater Treatment Plants.
  • In the short and medium term, information will be requested directly from operators of Wastewater Treatment Plants, as well as from DNP and CRA with the objective of knowing the management carried out regarding biogas and generating a diagnosis and baseline that allows identifying targets in the proposed horizons for the NDC or its subsequent updates.
  • Articulate regulatory frameworks to define financing alternatives and development of incentives that allow the development of biogas use and utilization projects from WWTPs.

2050

Consolidate a regulatory and operational framework that guarantees the efficient use of biogas, integrating technical, safety and environmental sustainability standards.

Cross-cutting approaches

In accordance with the Peace and Climate Action Methodological Matrix NDC 3.0, which integrates the five strategic Peace lines based on international metrics and aligned with the approaches of Just Transition, SDGs, Human Rights, Gender and Children, Alternative 2 – Implementation Enabling Mechanism is developed for this measure, linked mainly to Peace lines 3. Human Security and Community Resilience,

  • Governance and Transparency and 4. Sustainable Development and Positive Peace. Based on these lines, the measure aligns with:
  • Just Transition: Articulated with elements (a) and (c) of the JTWP by strengthening technical capacities, regulations and financial conditions for clean technologies in the sanitation sector, promoting energy efficiency and avoiding negative impacts on small providers and vulnerable communities.
  • Global SDGs: Aligned with SDGs 6.3.1, 7.2.1, 12.4.2 and 13.2.2, by improving sustainable water treatment, promoting clean energy, reducing pollutants and guaranteeing climate coherence in sectoral policies.
  • Human Rights: Contributes to equitable access to safe and sustainable sanitation services, promoting dignified living conditions and the principle of do no harm in communities. Additionally, it contributes to the realization of rights to health, access to potable water and strengthens the collective right to a healthy environment.
  • Gender: Promotes progress toward gender equality in accordance with the guide for the inclusion of the gender approach for women in the planning and budgeting cycle of the housing, city and territory sector, in which the Water and Basic Sanitation strategic line is established, proposing regulatory instruments for incorporating the gender approach in sectoral management of the right to water and access to basic sanitation. Taking into account the approaches set out in these normative instruments, the conditions and characteristics of people and groups are analyzed.
  • Children: The measure is articulated with the following MISNI indicators: DI9, DI13, JE2 and JE6. By being built through territorial participation and technical working groups, it allows girls, boys, adolescents and young people to be heard in decisions about the substitution of fossil fuels.

Analytical basis: Energy utilization of biogas in WWTPs reduces methane emissions, a high-impact GHG, and improves energy efficiency of the sanitation sector, strengthening water resilience and environmental sustainability. The measure promotes technical governance through regulatory guidelines, MRV and inter-institutional articulation, reduces sanitary risks and promotes circular economy through the use of treatment by-products. Its implementation benefits municipalities with coverage deficits, drives green employment and contributes to positive peace through improvements in territorial equity and environmental quality.

MEASURE NAME

DESCRIPTION

Low-carbon rural educational infrastructure that builds peace.

Private sector measures. FOOD BANKS..

Food waste contributes significantly to climate change, as it involves emissions derived from the production, transportation and final disposal of food that is not consumed, in addition to methane generation in sanitary landfills from the decomposition of organic matter. By rescuing and redistributing food suitable for human consumption, food banks prevent it from reaching landfills, reduce methane emissions and optimize the use of natural resources.

Food banks collect production surpluses, donations or food close to expiration, classify them and distribute them among vulnerable populations. In this way, in addition to their social impact, they contribute to climate change mitigation by reducing the carbon footprint of the food system and promoting circular economy models and responsible consumption.

Reduction Deforestation

INTERSectoral

DE

LA

Colombia recognizes that to reduce the change in area covered by natural forest due to deforestation to 50,000 ha by 2030, it must maintain and accelerate the actions established in national policy instruments for the reduction of deforestation and sustainable management of forests. The country maintains its ambition and commitment to containing deforestation, seeking by 2035 a rate between 37,500 and 49,999 ha at the national level.

Cross-cutting approaches. In accordance with the Peace and Climate Action Methodological Matrix NDC 3.0, which integrates the five strategic Peace lines based on international metrics and aligned with the approaches of Just Transition, SDGs, Human Rights, Gender and Children, Alternative 2 – Sustainable Productive Transformation Enabling Mechanism is developed for this measure, linked to Peace lines: 1. Governance and Transparency, 3. Human Security and Community Resilience, and 4. Sustainable Development and Positive Peace.

  • Just Transition – Element (b) of the UNFCCC Just Transition Work Programme, related to socioeconomic transition pathways based on national priorities, social protection and structural actions to address deforestation drivers.
  • Global SDGs 15.1.1, 15.2.1, 16.6.2 and 13.2.2, oriented toward forest conservation, sustainable management, institutional transparency and climate coherence in public policies.
  • Human Rights: Right to a healthy environment, right to sustainable development, right to the sustainable use of natural resources, within the framework of the right to Free, Prior and Informed Consultation, participation, as well as the Rights of Indigenous Peoples, Black, Afro-Colombian, Raizal and Palenquero communities, and peasant communities.
  • Gender: Involvement of Indigenous and peasant women's associations. Visibility of women's role in effective participation processes in decision-making on actions for reducing deforestation in accordance with the Biodiversity Action Plan to 2030, which promotes cross-cutting gender actions for the recognition of women, communities and territories based on the actions developed within the framework of the 6 targets that contribute to fulfilling the 23 global targets of the Kunming-Montreal Framework.
  • Children: The measure is articulated with the following MISNI indicators: DI9, DI13, JE2, JE5 and JE6. By strengthening community forest governance, it opens spaces for girls, boys and adolescents to be heard and influence decisions about the forest and territory (DI9, JE2).

Analytical basis: This measure constitutes a structural governance action to contain deforestation through multi-sectoral instruments, strengthening of the State in the territory and cooperation with communities. It contributes to positive peace by reducing illegal economies associated with conflicts, drives territorial cohesion, protects strategic natural heritage and guarantees the rights of local populations, women and children, through co-responsibility and inclusive governance.

MEASURE NAME

Manejo Forestal Sustainable Comunitario

DESCRIPTION

HITOS

  • By 2030 in areas with permits for the persistent forest use of timber and non-timber products, reach 10,000 (ha) of natural forest in the process of implementing the SFMC with assessment of degradation level and quantification of emission reductions.
  • Climate finance mechanisms developed, including community-level carbon initiatives, payments for environmental services in forest projects and productive incentives for the transformation of forest goods and services with added value.
  • Monitoring, reporting and verification (MRV) system based on permanent forest inventories, remote sensing systems and participatory monitoring, operated in coordination with the National Forest Inventory and the National Environmental System.
  • Management and restoration protocols for degraded forests, with criteria for evaluating different degradation levels.
  • Information for the baseline of emission reductions from natural forest management and enrichment processes.
  • National guidelines and territorial articulation (Environmental Authorities + communities) based on the strengthening of the National Roundtable on Community Sustainable Forest Management.

Annex 4. Adaptation Goals and Measures, and

Loss and Damage

Adaptation Goals and Measures

This section gathers the minimum adaptation climate commitments that Colombia assumes within the framework of its Third Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC 3.0). These commitments, in the form of adaptation targets and measures, have been structured to directly respond to the strategic adaptation and climate resilience objectives defined in the Second Adaptation Communication (AdCom), thereby ensuring their coherence with the Global Adaptation Goal (GAG), the National Adaptation Plan and international adaptation frameworks.

This annex is an integral part of NDC 3.0 and seeks to facilitate traceability between national priorities and sectoral and territorial actions. The portfolio consists of 42 adaptation climate commitments, each with its respective target, responsible entity, support needs (financing, capacities, technology, planning, governance and others), and applicable cross-cutting approaches.

Colombia reaffirms that climate change adaptation action is not limited to policy formulation, but translates into clear, measurable and verifiable targets, which enable resource mobilization and inter-sectoral and territorial articulation. The set of commitments presented here consequently constitutes the basis for the monitoring, reporting and evaluation of the adaptation that the country will advance at the national and international level, in compliance with its obligations before the UNFCCC and the Paris Agreement.

The Annex is organized around nine national adaptation priorities and for each one the associated sectoral climate commitments are presented. These commitments are structured in fact sheets that record their agreed wording, the responsible party in terms of coordination and reporting, the strategic allies for planning, implementation and monitoring, the support needs, and the relevant SIIVRA dimensions for impact monitoring.

1. Biodiversity and Ecosystem Services

Increase the adaptive capacity of terrestrial and marine-coastal ecosystems to sustain and restore ecosystem services (water regulation, flood protection, connectivity) through conservation, restoration and Ecosystem-based Adaptation (EbA) actions integrated into territorial planning.

ENVIRONMENT SECTOR

Climate Commitment NDC3A01:

Title: Expansion of the ecological representativeness of SINAP by 2030. Adaptation target: Increase by 2030, by 15% relative to the 2021 representativeness baseline, the ecosystems or ecosystemic analysis units that are not represented or under-represented in the National Protected Areas System (SINAP). Responsible Entity: National Natural Parks of Colombia (PNNC). Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DBBSE).

Strategic allies: Regional Autonomous Corporations (CAR). Institutes of the National Environmental System (SINA). National Planning Department. Urban Environmental Authorities.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Funds from the environmental budget and international cooperation (GEF, GCF, bilateral cooperation, and others).
  • Resources for land purchase and declaration of new protected areas.
  • Co-financing with innovative financial mechanisms (water funds, payments for environmental services, etc.).
  • Resources from CONPES Policy 4050.

2. Capacities:

  • Capacity building to generate an updated map of conservation priorities to adjust and refine national and regional goals related to ecosystem representativeness.
  • Formación de comunidades locales y grupos étnicos en gobernanza de áreas protegidas y restauración.
  • Training in methodologies for identifying conservation priorities and analysis of representativeness and ecological connectivity.

3. Technology:

  • GIS tools and ecological modelling for identifying conservation priorities at regional and national scale.
  • Integration of results into SIIVRA for monitoring.
  • Innovation in SINAP information systems to measure progress on representativeness and connectivity.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Coordination with the Biodiversity Action Plan, CONPES 4050 and other conservation policies.
  • Coordinación multinivel entre Ambiente, PNNC, CAR, autoridades locales y comunidades.
  • Participación de grupos étnicos, comunidades locales y sociedad civil en procesos de declaratoria y manejo.
  • Promoción de las figuras de conservación de reservas naturales de la sociedad civil – RNSC (áreas protegidas privadas) por parte de las autoridades ambientales entre otros, como apoyo en la consolidación de la representatividad ecológica y conectividad.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: No aplica.

Climate Commitment NDC3A02:

Title: Rehabilitation, recovery or restoration in areas of the National Parks System by 2030. Adaptation target: Increase by 2030, by 100,000 hectares, the areas in the process of rehabilitation, recovery and restoration in the areas of the National Parks System and their zones of influence.

Responsible Entities: National Natural Parks of Colombia (PNNC). Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DBBSE and DAMCRA).

Strategic allies: Regional Autonomous Corporations. National Environmental Licensing Authority (ANLA). SINA Institutes.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Resources from the Fund for Life.
  • Presupuesto General de la Nación
  • International cooperation (GEF, GCF, bilateral cooperation) through the Heritage Colombia Program (Permanence Financing Project — PFP) as mechanisms that connect financing supply and demand and channel resources to territories.
  • Co-financing with environmental and sectoral funds for restoration.
  • Compensación por pérdida de biodiversidad y 1%.
  • Incentivos financieros y esquemas de PSA (pagos por servicios ambientales) para comunidades vinculadas.

2. Capacities:

  • Training for park rangers, local communities and ethnic peoples in ecological restoration techniques.
  • Institutional strengthening of PNNC and Regional Autonomous Corporations for monitoring ecological restoration processes.

3. Technology:

  • GIS tools and satellite monitoring to prioritize and verify areas undergoing restoration.
  • Integración con el SIIVRA – componente de biodiversidad (SIAC/SNICC/SNIF) para evaluar contribución de la restauración.
  • Innovación en técnicas de restauración y propagación de especies vegetales nativas.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Coordination with the Biodiversity Action Plan, National Restoration Plan (NRP), PNNC management plans and other conservation policies.
  • Multilevel coordination between PNNC, the CAR, territorial entities and communities.
  • Participación de grupos étnicos y comunidades locales en los procesos de restauración.
  • Acuerdos de conservación y restauración con comunidades.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: No aplica.

Climate Commitment NDC3A03:

Title: Incorporation of climate change in ANLA's environmental management and control instruments by 2030. Adaptation measure: By 2030, climate change considerations are included in 100% of the environmental management and control instruments of projects, works and/or activities in which their application is identified and that are within the competence of the National Environmental Licensing Authority (ANLA), corresponding to new environmental licenses and modifications to licenses or equivalent instruments.

Strategic partners: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DAASU) Other SISCLIMA ministries Corporaciones Autónomas Regional (CAR) Institutos SINA

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • National resources for hiring personnel to update ANLA's management instruments and systems.
  • International cooperation for technical assistance in mainstreaming climate change into environmental assessment and monitoring processes for environmental licenses.

2. Capacities:

  • Training of ANLA and CAR officials in applying adaptation and GHG mitigation criteria.
  • Development of technical guides/manuals/tools specific to each type of control instrument.

3. Technology:

  • Interoperable digital platforms between ANLA, IDEAM and Ambiente for tracking licenses and plans.
  • Integration with SIIVRA and SNICC to report contributions to climate change planning and management instruments.
  • Herramientas para la toma de decisiones ambientales con enfoque climático.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Adoption of instruments for the assessment and monitoring of EAPs subject to environmental licensing, incorporating climate criteria.
  • Coordination with DNP and productive sectors to ensure regulatory coherence.
  • Participación de la sociedad civil y comunidades en la veeduría de procesos de control ambiental.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Gestión del Riesgo de Desastres.

  • Global SDGs: Contributes to indicators 13.2.1 (climate integration in policies and planning), 15.9.1 (integration of environmental values in planning systems) and 16.7.1 (inclusive and participatory decision-making).
  • Human Rights: guarantee of access to information.
  • Gender: The implementation of this measure in accordance with the ANLA participation strategy, which includes a differential and gender approach establishing that actions developed will understand and address existing inequalities and gaps in terms of gender, ethnicity, age, disability status and other characteristics to guarantee equal opportunities and equitable access to participation especially for groups in conditions of vulnerability.

Children (UNICEF Global – Child-sensitive NDCs): Contributes to indicators JE2,

DI9 and JE9, by promoting healthy environments, access to environmental information and intergenerational participation in decisions about projects affecting ecosystems and territories.

Analytical basis:

This measure strengthens the country's environmental management by ensuring that all management and control instruments evaluated by ANLA integrate climate change considerations coherently with the cross-cutting approaches of human rights and gender. By incorporating these criteria, decision-making will be oriented toward more resilient projects with greater adaptive capacity.

Climate Commitment NDC3A06:

Title:

Comprehensive adaptation initiatives in mangrove ecosystems

Adaptation target:

By 2030, implement at least six comprehensive climate change adaptation and risk management initiatives in mangrove ecosystems, oriented toward their sustainable use, reduction of local vulnerabilities and conservation of key ecosystem services.

Responsible entities:

Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DAMCRA)

Strategic partners:

National Disaster Risk Management Unit (UNGRD). Regional Autonomous Corporations (CAR). Corporations for Sustainable Development. National Natural Parks of Colombia (PNNC). Institutes of the National Environmental System (SINA). Governorships and mayor's offices.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • National budget for expansion and maintenance of monitoring stations.
  • International cooperation (IDB, WB, GCF, bilateral cooperation, and others) for monitoring technologies and EWS.
  • Territorial funds for installation of stations in critical areas.

2. Capacities:

  • Training of IDEAM, CAR and municipalities in operation and maintenance of stations.
  • Strengthening of UNGRD, IDEAM and local authorities in management of EWS.
  • Community training in use of information and protocols for early response protocols.

3. Technology:

  • Modernization of monitoring stations with real-time transmission.
  • Interoperable digital platforms with SIIVRA to record and analyze data.
  • Integration of multi-hazard EWS with climate scenarios.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Inclusion of the target in PNACC and in territorial risk management plans.
  • Inter-institutional coordination (Environment, IDEAM, UNGRD, CAR, mayor's offices).
  • Participation of local communities in community EWS processes.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Disaster Risk Management.

Cross-cutting approaches and elements:

In accordance with the Peace and Climate Action Methodological Matrix NDC 3.0, which integrates the five strategic Peace lines based on international metrics, each of them aligned with the cross-cutting approaches of Just Transition, SDGs, Human Rights, Gender and Children, Alternative 2 – Implementation Enabling Mechanism is developed, linked to Peace lines 3. Human Security and Community Resilience and 4. Sustainable Development and Positive Peace.

● Element (d): of the UNFCCC Just Transition Work Programme, aimed at strengthening adaptation and climate resilience through coastal ecosystems. ● Global SDGs 13.1, 14.2, 14.5 and 15.1: drive risk reduction, mangrove restoration and conservation of essential ecosystem services. ● Human Rights: principles of participation, access to information and protection of the territory against climate threats (Escazú), as well as recognition of community rights.

Analytical basis:

This measure reinforces the adaptive capacity of coastal communities, improves climate risk management and guarantees the functional conservation of mangroves through institutional cooperation, scientific monitoring and social participation.

Human Rights and Social Empowerment, 3. Human Security and Community Resilience and 4. Sustainable Development and Positive Peace.

● Children: drives the JE2, DI9 and DI13 indicators of UNICEF Global

Child-sensitive NDCs, related to intergenerational participation, capacity building in coastal and marine communities and guarantees the right to a healthy and safe environment.

Analytical basis:

The measure contributes to participatory environmental governance, conservation of strategic ecosystems and territorial equity. Through the expansion of protected areas and community management, it strengthens the ecological and social resilience of the country, guaranteeing direct benefits in biodiversity, climate adaptation and social cohesion.

Climate Commitment NDC3A08:

Title: Colombia 30x30: 30% of territory under protection figures before 2030. Adaptation target: By 2030, at least [30%] of the national territory (continental and marine) will be under protection or complementary conservation figures, with active management processes that contribute to biodiversity conservation and adaptation to climate change. Responsible entities: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DAMCRA and DBBSE).

Strategic allies: Regional Autonomous Corporations (CAR). National Natural Parks of Colombia (PNNC). SINA Institutes. Governorships and mayor's offices.

  • International cooperation (GEF, GCF, bilateral and other cooperation) for conservation and restoration.
  • Incentivos económicos y PSA (pagos por servicios ambientales) para comunidades en áreas de conservación complementaria.

  • Institutional strengthening of PNNC, the CAR and Ambiente for participatory management.
  • Community training in governance and adaptive conservation.

3. Technology:

  • Satellite monitoring of coverage and conservation status.
  • Integración con SIIVRA para evaluar impacto en resiliencia ecosistémica.
  • Innovación en metodologías de conservación basadas en SbN.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Inclusión de la meta en el SINAP, planes de ordenamiento ambiental y PIGCCT.
  • Multilevel coordination (national, regional, local) with respect to the Biodiversity Action Plan.
  • Participation of ethnic peoples, local communities and civil society in declaration and management.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: No aplica.

Climate Commitment NDC3A09:

Title: Restoration and water resilience in prioritized páramo and high Andean forest ecosystems. Adaptation target: By 2030, reduce vulnerability and strengthen the socio-ecological resilience of the páramo and high Andean forest ecosystems of Chingaza, Guerrero and Santurbán or other páramos against the degradation and loss of water services associated with climate change and variability, through the implementation of Nature-based Solutions oriented toward restoration and conservation.

Strategic allies: Regional Autonomous Corporations (CAR, CORPONOR, CDMB, CORPOBOYACÁ). Bogotá Water Fund – Agua Somos Corporation. Santander and Norte de Santander Water Fund – Biocuenca Alliance Foundation.

Sectoral ally: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DCCGR and DGIRH). Ministry of Commerce, Industry and Tourism.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Financing for technological innovation and bioengineering applied to restoration. Co-financing from local communities for maintenance of restored areas and PSE management.
  • Coordination with international cooperation financing programs such as the Green Climate Fund páramo corridor, among others.
  • Environmental Services Payment Mechanisms (PSA) for watershed protection and development of climate adaptation solutions.

2. Capacities:

  • Technical strengthening of CAR and rural communities in ecological restoration, participatory monitoring and adaptive management.
  • Training of corporate teams in climate management, Nature-based Solutions for water security and development of community ecotourism programs.
  • Institutional strengthening for coordination between the private sector and national adaptation policies.

3. Technology:

  • GIS tools, hydrological modelling and interoperable platforms with SIIVRA/SNICC for monitoring the effectiveness of measures.
  • Innovación en técnicas de restauración y soluciones naturales de retención de agua.
  • Monitoring of coverage and ecological functionality through satellite imagery, sensors and drones.
  • Early warning and risk monitoring systems.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Strengthening of territorial governance and local participation mechanisms.
  • Interinstitutional coordination with PNACC, the National Water Plan and POMCA Plans.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Food Security and Agricultural Production; Disaster Risk.

  • Global SDGs: Contributes especially to SDGs 6.6, 13.1, 15.1 and 15.4, by protecting water services, restoring strategic high-altitude ecosystems and strengthening the adaptive capacity of communities. Contributes to SDG 17.16 due to its high component of multi-level partnerships between private sector, water funds, CARs and local communities.
  • Human Rights: The preservation of páramo and forest ecosystems and essential water resources guarantees the protection of rights to life, health, access to drinking water, a healthy environment, food and sustainable development for Andean communities; in addition, promotes the right to participation, self-determination and access to financing.
  • Gender: Reduce vulnerability and strengthen the socio-ecological resilience of people who care for and/or inhabit the páramo and high Andean forest ecosystems of Chingaza, Guerrero and Santurbán or other páramos in accordance with observation 15 of the Committee on Economic, Social and Cultural Rights (CESCR) on equitable access to water and water management systems.
  • Children (MISNI): Contributes to UNICEF Colombia indicators of child-sensitive NDCs

(J. H)

Analytical basis

The measure protects high-altitude ecosystems fundamental for water security, livelihoods and the country's climate resilience. By driving restoration, territorial governance and community participation under NbS, it strengthens human security, social cohesion and positive peace in strategic regions, integrating climate action, rights protection and ecological sustainability.

MINING AND ENERGY SECTOR

Climate Commitment NDC3A10:

Title: Conservation and restoration actions in strategic ecosystems in Colombia. Adaptation measure: By 2030, implement conservation and ecological restoration actions in at least 500 hectares of prioritized strategic ecosystems, including recovery, remediation and rehabilitation activities, in order to reduce territorial vulnerability and strengthen resilience to climate change, through interventions developed under the Nature-Based Solutions approach.

Strategic allies: Ministry of Mines and Energy (MME). Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (MinAmbiente) – DBBSE and DCCGR. Regional and Sustainable Development Autonomous Corporations (CARs). Territorial entities and local communities linked to conservation processes. NGOs: WCS, Fondo Acción, Fundación Natura, Fundación Cataruben (Strategic allies).

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Promote technical and regulatory enablers to facilitate access to financing, together with mobilization of business resources and national and international climate cooperation, to implement conservation, restoration and climate adaptation strategies with community participation. Coordination with climate financing mechanisms (e.g., bilateral or multilateral cooperation).

2. Capacities:

  • Standardize registration protocols with homogeneous indicators on recovery, remediation, conservation, rehabilitation and ecological restoration, ecosystem status, monitoring and results.
  • Strengthening of institutional and community capacities in adaptive ecosystem management, participatory monitoring and citizen science.

3. Technology:

  • Interoperable platforms for sharing information among public and private actors.
  • Uso del SIIVRA y escenarios climáticos del IDEAM para priorizar vulnerabilidades.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Promote interinstitutional coordination with IDEAM, DNP and IGAC to cross-reference geospatial and monitoring information.
  • Consolidation of interinstitutional and sectoral mechanisms for the sustainable management of recovery, remediation, conservation, rehabilitation and ecological restoration areas, promoting coherence between business and national adaptation goals.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Hábitat humano, Riesgo de Desastres.

  • Just Transition (Decision 3/CMA.5): Contributes to element (d) by strengthening Ecosystem-based Adaptation, (f) for its emphasis on community participation and territorial articulation, and (g) by requiring technical and financial cooperation for restoration, monitoring and institutional strengthening.
  • Global SDGs: Related to SDGs 13.1, 14.2, 15.1 and 15.3 by improving climate resilience, conserving strategic ecosystems and promoting sustainable land management. Contributes to SDG 16.7 through participatory and co-governance processes.
  • Human Rights: By reducing the vulnerability of territories, this measure contributes to the protection of fundamental rights such as life, health, a healthy environment, access to water, and food; at the same time, promotes the protection of the rights of the most vulnerable communities, particularly peasant, indigenous and Afro-descendant communities and victims of climate damage, from a participatory and self-determination approach.
  • Gender: Develop actions to reduce the vulnerability of women and men in the face of climate change by generating conditions for the resilience of people and territories in accordance with what is proposed in Ecopetrol's diversity, equity and inclusion program within the framework of the commitments of the mining and energy sector in CONPES 4080 and the Gender and Climate Change Action Plan in relation to public-private partnerships for gender equity.
  • Children: The measure is articulated with the following MISNI indicators: DI5, DI10, DI13 and JE9.

By reducing biodiversity losses and waste and improving conservation management, it decreases pollution and sanitary risks, protecting the health of children and adolescents in urban and rural environments, ensuring a healthy and safe environment.

Analytical basis

The measure strengthens the resilience of territories through ecological restoration and socio-environmental governance, reduces vulnerabilities and improves key ecosystem services. Its participatory and NbS-based nature makes it a central enabler for human security, positive peace and climate adaptation in vulnerable regions.

2. Water Resources

Reduce the risk of urban and rural water supply disruption to guarantee resilient water and sanitation through integrated management in supply watersheds, efficiency in water systems and supply-demand measures prioritized in vulnerable territories.

WATER AND BASIC SANITATION SECTOR

Climate Commitment NDC3A11:

Title: Protection and conservation of micro-watersheds. Adaptation target: Develop by 2030 protection and conservation actions in twenty-four (24) supply micro-watersheds of water systems in municipalities susceptible to supply disruption during low rainfall and rainy season periods. Responsible entities: Ministry of Housing, City and Territory (Vice-Ministry of Water and Basic Sanitation).

Strategic allies: Territorial Entities. Public Household Service Providers. Environmental Authorities. Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DGIRH).

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Resources from the national and territorial budget for watershed protection and conservation.
  • Access to international funds for water and resilience projects.
  • Incentivos financieros a prestadores, comunidades y asociaciones que participen en conservación.
  • Reinvestment of carbon tax resources for adaptation.

2. Capacities:

  • Strengthen the technical capacity of local actors in integrated water resource management.
  • Strengthen the capacities of sectoral actors for climate change management.

3. Technology:

  • Use of IDEAM hydrometeorological information for prioritization of vulnerable watersheds.
  • Plataformas digitales interoperables con el SIIVRA para monitorear avances.
  • Innovación en técnicas de restauración y conservación hídrica.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Coordination with other territorial planning instruments such as POT, POMCA and PIGCCT.
  • Coordination with Regional Autonomous Corporations for Sustainable Development and regional climate change nodes.
  • Participación de comunidades locales y pueblos étnicos en procesos de conservación.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Hábitat Humano, Infraestructura.

Climate Commitment NDC3A12:

Title: Adaptation actions for municipalities susceptible to water supply disruption. Adaptation target: Develop by 2030 structural and non-structural risk management actions for climate change adaptation in thirty percent (30%) of municipalities prioritized for susceptibility to supply disruption during dry and rainy seasons. Responsible entities: Ministry of Housing, City and Territory (Vice-Ministry of Water and Basic Sanitation).

Strategic allies: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DGIRH and DAASU). Institute of Hydrology, Meteorology and Environmental Studies (IDEAM). National Disaster Risk Management Unit (UNGRD). Territorial Entities. Public Household Service Providers. Environmental Authorities.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • National and territorial budget resources for water infrastructure and early warning systems (SAT).
  • Access to international funds for sectoral adaptation projects.
  • Cofinanciación con esquemas de acueductos comunitarios y asociaciones de usuarios.

2. Capacities:

  • Technical training for local officials in drought, flood and water resilience management.
  • Institutional strengthening of municipalities and governorships in climate risk planning.
  • Development of community preparedness and response protocols.

3. Technology:

  • Herramientas de modelación climática del IDEAM para priorizar municipios de alta vulnerabilidad.
  • Plataformas interoperables con el SIIVRA para monitorear reducción de riesgo.
  • Innovation in water harvesting and storage technologies adapted to rural and urban contexts.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Inclusión en POT, POMCA y PIGCCT de municipios priorizados.
  • Coordination with UNGRD and regional climate change nodes.
  • Active participation of local communities, water supply boards and ethnic peoples in implementation.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: .

Climate Commitment NDC3A14:

Title: Reuse of domestic wastewater. Adaptation target: Reuse by 2035 ten percent (10%) of treated domestic wastewater by providers of the sewerage public service, for non-potable purposes, in compliance with quality standards and current regulations, contributing to water resilience in municipalities. Responsible entities: Ministry of Housing, City and Territory.

Strategic allies: Territorial Entities. Public Household Service Providers. Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DAASU). Superintendency of Public Household Services. Environmental Authorities.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Public and private resources for treated water reuse infrastructure.
  • Incentivos financieros a prestadores que adopten prácticas de reúso.
  • International funds for technological innovation in reuse.

2. Capacities:

  • Technical training for water supply operators in reuse technologies. Training of local officials in alternative water resource management.
  • Sensibilización comunitaria sobre seguridad y beneficios del reúso.

3. Technology:

  • Innovation in advanced and modular wastewater treatment systems.
  • Water quality and regulatory compliance monitoring platforms.
  • Vinculación con el SIIVRA para registrar aportes a la resiliencia hídrica.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Coordination with the basic sanitation and drinking water policy and the PIGCCS for Housing (water and basic sanitation).
  • Coordination with the CRA to establish technical and regulatory standards.
  • Coordinación con municipios para priorizar usos y territorios de aplicación.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Hábitat Humano, Infraestructura.

  • Human Rights: guarantees equitable access to alternative water resources and public information on the quality of reused water.

Gender: in coherence with the UNFCCC Gender Action Plan, promotes the participation of women in technological innovation and decision-making on water reuse in urban communities.

  • Children: drives indicators JE2, DI9 and JE17 of UNICEF Global

Child-sensitive NDCs, related to environmental education, youth leadership and intergenerational co-responsibility in sustainable practices.

Analytical basis:

This measure promotes the circular economy of water as a strategy of adaptation and water efficiency, integrating technological innovation, social equity and environmental sustainability. It contributes to urban and rural water security, reducing pressure on natural sources and strengthening the climate resilience of municipalities.

ENVIRONMENT SECTOR

Climate Commitment NDC3A15:

Title: POMCA with climate change considerations. Adaptation measure: Formulate or adjust by 2030, a minimum of one hundred and thirty-five (135) River Basin Management Plans (POMCA) incorporating climate variability and change considerations, in accordance with the prioritization established by the Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development.

Responsible entities: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DGIRH)

Strategic allies: Regional Environmental Authorities (AAR). Institute of Hydrology, Meteorology and Environmental Studies (IDEAM). National Planning Department (DNP). Ministry of Interior – Prior Consultation Authority. Governorships and Mayor's offices.

  • National and regional environmental budget resources for POMCA formulation and updating.
  • Co-financing with international cooperation and water funds.
  • Financial incentives for projects derived from climate-adjusted POMCA.

  • Technical training in incorporating climate variables in watershed management.
  • Strengthening of technical teams in CAR and environmental authorities.

3. Technology:

  • Hydrological and climate modelling tools for prioritizing measures.
  • Traceability platforms and integration of IDEAM and SIIVRA information in watershed diagnostics.
  • Innovation in participatory methodologies for water resource management.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Coordination of POMCA with POT and PIGCCT.
  • Coordination between the Ministry of Environment, Regional Environmental Authorities, territorial entities and Indigenous Authorities.
  • Inclusión de enfoques diferenciales (pueblos étnicos, comunidades rurales).

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Gestión del Riesgo de Desastres.

Climate Commitment NDC3A18:

Title: Increase in water availability for irrigation and domestic use. Adaptation target: By 2030, implement rainwater harvesting programs (120,000 m³) in the jurisdiction of the CAR to increase the availability of water for irrigation and domestic use in order to reduce the impact of drought periods. Responsible entities: Regional Autonomous Corporation of Cundinamarca (CAR Cundinamarca).

Strategic partners: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DGIRH)

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • International cooperation (IDB, WB, CAF, GCF) for adaptation pilot projects on rainwater capture and harvesting.

2. Capacities:

  • Technical training programs aimed at communities and irrigation user associations on basic maintenance, simple water treatment (domestic use) and management of the quality of harvested water.

3. Technology:

  • Implementation of low-cost sensors or devices for real-time or periodic monitoring of the volume of harvested water and the level of storage, integrating this information into the National System of Information on Climate Change (SNICC) for monitoring the target.
  • Use of climate services (EWS, modeling) to feed back adaptation indicators.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Community and territorial participation in monitoring that allows reporting progress of the 120,000 m³, demonstrating the impact on the reduction of vulnerability.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Food Security and Agricultural Production, Infrastructure.

Cross-cutting approaches and elements:

In accordance with the Peace and Climate Action Methodological Matrix NDC 3.0, Alternative 2 – Implementation Enabling Mechanism is developed, linked to Peace lines 3. Human Security and Community Resilience and 4. Sustainable Development and Positive Peace.

● Element (d) of the UNFCCC Just Transition Work Programme: resilience against extreme events through adaptive and community infrastructure. ● SDGs 6.4, 11.5, 12.2 and 13.1: promote water efficiency, risk reduction and productive sustainability. ● Human Rights: guarantees the right to water in rural contexts and equitable access to natural resources. ● Gender: In accordance with observation 15 of the Committee on Economic, Social and Cultural Rights (CESCR)

● Children: activates indicators DI5, DI9, JE2 and JE3 of UNICEF Global

Child-sensitive NDCs, which guarantees equitable access to water and sanitary services, youth participation and innovation in sustainable practices.

Analytical basis:

This measure increases the availability and efficient management of water in rural territories through innovation, participatory governance and local cooperation. It strengthens community and productive resilience against droughts, driving environmental justice and food security.

Climate Commitment NDC3A19:

Title: Conservation of supply watersheds. Adaptation target: By 2030, implement a Payment for Environmental Services (PES) scheme in supply watersheds of the department of Cundinamarca, in order to conserve 20,000 hectares of strategic ecosystems and strengthen water regulation in prioritized municipalities. Responsible entities: Cundinamarca Governorate.

Strategic allies: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (ONVS and DGIRH). Regional Autonomous Corporations (CAR). National Natural Parks of Colombia (PNNC). SINA Institutes. Governorships and mayor's offices.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Co-financing with international cooperation and water funds.
  • Financial incentives for projects derived from climate-adjusted POMCA.

2. Capacities:

  • Training for the beneficiaries/custodians of the 20,000 hectares in sustainable management practices, ecological restoration, agroforestry or silvopastoral systems (depending on the ecosystem), so that conservation is not passive but productive and active.

3. Technology:

  • Implementation of technological tools (satellite imagery, drones, mobile applications) for remote monitoring and verification that the 20,000 hectares remain conserved, reducing field visit costs.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Technical support to conduct a detailed Water Vulnerability Analysis in Cundinamarca to select sub-watersheds and the 20,000 hectares where the impact on water regulation will be maximum under climate change scenarios.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Biodiversidad y servicios ecosistémicos.

AGRICULTURAL SECTOR

Climate Commitment NDC3A20:

Title: Incorporation of climate considerations in agricultural planning and adaptation to Adaptation target: Adopt by 2030 climate change considerations in the planning instruments of the agricultural sector (PIGCCS) and implement adaptation actions. Title: Expansion of coverage and participation in agroclimatic technical roundtables by 2030. Climate Commitment NDC3A23:

Title: National agroecological transition for adaptation and food sovereignty. Adaptation target: By 2035, at least 50% of agricultural and forest supply chains will have initiated and documented climate-smart agroecological transition processes, incorporating regenerative soil practices, productive diversification, biological control and conservation of functional biodiversity, with monitoring and verification mechanisms.

This transition approach will promote productive restoration and the conservation of key ecosystem services (pollinators, biological controllers, ecological connectivity and agroforestry corridors) and the strengthening of peasant, family, ethnic and community agriculture. Both aspects as the basis of agroecological resilience and productivity. Responsible entities: Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development.

Strategic allies: Colombian Corporation for Agricultural Research (Agrosavia). Rural Agricultural Planning Unit (UPRA).

Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (MADS), Universities, organizations, ACFEC, Ministry of Mines and Energy

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Cofinanciación público-privada con gremios y organizaciones de cadena.
  • Access to international cooperation for technology transfer (e.g., FAO, IDB, World Bank, EU).
  • Budget to implement plans and programs to promote agroecological practices.
  • Incentives for agroecological projects that integrate adaptation standards.
  • Promover la creación de líneas verdes de crédito y fondos climáticos (como el FENOGE y el Fondo Colombia Sostenible) para apalancar prácticas de mitigación de GEI en la agricultura campesina, familiar y comunitaria.

2. Capacities:

  • Creación de redes de aprendizaje entre subsectores para intercambio de experiencias.
  • Development of technical, social and organizational capacities for promoters, extension workers and rural communities through training programs in agroecology, climate resilience and bioinput management.
  • Fortalecer las Escuelas Agroecológicas y la red de promotorías campesinas y étnicas como espacios de aprendizaje y acción climática local.
  • Fortalecer las prácticas productivas de la agricultura campesina, familiar, étnica y comunitaria.

3. Technology:

  • Implementation of agroecological technologies for integrated soil, water and biodiversity management: biofertilizers, biocontrollers, agroforestry systems, clean energy and digital tools for soil and emissions monitoring.
  • Impulso a la investigación aplicada y a la transferencia tecnológica a través del SNIA, AGROSAVIA y las universidades.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Participación de asociaciones campesinas, indígenas y afrodescendientes en la definición y validación de indicadores de resiliencia.
  • Consolidate the National Agroecology Board as a governance body for the Public Agroecology Policy.
  • Municipal and Departmental Agroecology Bodies (producer associations with agroecological processes, agroecological organizations, Secretariats of Agriculture, Autonomous Corporations, National entities with territorial presence, delegates from participation bodies linked to the National System for Agrarian Reform and Rural Development (CMDR, Municipal Committees for Agrarian Reform)).
  • Promote territorial planning instruments with an agroecological approach (Farm-Level Agroecological Transition Plans – IPPTA) and their coordination with Territorial Integrated Climate Change Management Plans (PIGCCT) and Development Plans with Territorial Focus (PDET).
  • Asegurar el componente de transición agroecológica en los planes de ordenamiento productivo y en las instancias de gobernanza de los fondos parafiscales.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: No Aplica.

  • Just Transition: Contributes to element (a) by promoting the transition toward sustainable productive systems based on agroecology; to element (d) through strengthening of technical capacities for ecological soil management, bio-inputs and diversification; and to element (f) for its participatory and community approach in the transition toward low-emission production models.
  • Global SDGs: Articulates with SDGs 2.4.1, 6.4.1, 12.4.1, 12.5.1 and 13.2.2, by promoting sustainable agricultural production, reduction of synthetic inputs, regenerative soil management, circular economy and climate coherence in the agroecological transition.
  • Gender: This measure will be developed in accordance with the gender commitments in the PAGCC-CO and with the differential approach framework of the PIGCCS of the agricultural sector which establishes that climate change management takes into account that vulnerability reduction must be considered from a gender perspective and intersectionally, recognizing the need to advance in the elimination of poverty and hunger, gender equality and the adoption of measures to combat climate change
  • Children: Articulates with MISNI indicators DI13, JE5 and JE9, by improving soils, biodiversity and environmental quality for girls, boys and adolescents; strengthening climate justice in vulnerable territories; and promoting youth empowerment through training and participation in agroecological practices and rural innovation.

Analytical basis:

Agroecology reduces emissions through substitution of external inputs, soil restoration and productive diversification, strengthening climate resilience, environmental health and sustainability of rural livelihoods. By consolidating regenerative and community productive systems, the measure contributes to human security, sustainable productive development and positive peace in vulnerable agricultural territories.

4. Human Health

Increase adaptive capacity and health resilience to reduce climate-sensitive morbidity and mortality through operational capacity of the health system, early surveillance, heat management and hydrometeorological events, integrated management of vectors and agents, with a focus on vulnerable populations.

HEALTH AND SOCIAL PROTECTION SECTOR

Climate Commitment NDC3A24:

Title: Actions to reduce cases of climate-sensitive diseases. Adaptation measure: Formulate by 2030 adaptation actions for disease prevention and health promotion, that contribute to reducing cases of climate-sensitive diseases in one hundred percent (100%) of health sector entities at departmental, district and municipal level. Additionally, by 2030 actions will be implemented in forty percent (40%) of said entities. Responsible entities:

Strategic allies: National Health Institute (INS). Institute of Hydrology, Meteorology and Environmental Studies (IDEAM). Departmental, district and municipal health secretariats (categories 1, 2 and 3).

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Additional budget for the health sector through international cooperation (PAHO, WHO, GCF, and others).
  • Specific funds to strengthen health systems in vulnerable municipalities.
  • Budget of territorial entities, such as royalties, transfers or own resources.
  • Territorial co-financing for climate-resilient health projects.
  • Support for NGO projects involving climate and health issues.

2. Capacities:

  • Formación de equipos de salud pública en identificación y prevención de enfermedades sensibles al clima.
  • Strengthening of health secretariats in climate planning and response.
  • Training of EPS/IPS in sanitary adaptation protocols.
  • Capacity building for members of the Sectoral Committee on Climate Change

3. Technology:

  • Integración de servicios climáticos
  • from IDEAM in epidemiological alert systems.
  • Interoperable platforms with SIIVRA for monitoring health impacts.
  • Digital tools for prediction and surveillance of climate-sensitive diseases (e.g., dengue, malaria, heat stroke). For example, strengthening and updating the climate and health bulletin.
  • Health early warning systems with a territorial approach.
  • Development and implementation of community surveillance systems for climate-sensitive events.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Inclusion in the goals of Territorial Climate Change Adaptation Plans from Health in harmony with the Territorial Integrated Climate Change Management Plans.
  • Inclusion of targets in the Health PIGCCS and territorial health plans.
  • Intersectoral coordination (health, environment, water, sanitation).
  • Participación comunitaria y enfoque diferencial (poblaciones rurales, étnicas y en pobreza extrema).
  • Include technical strengthening in university academic programs (with support from the education sector) and in the induction and reinduction processes of health secretariats.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Seguridad Alimentaria, Recursos Hídricos, Biodiversidad y Servicios.

Climate Commitment NDC3A25:

Title: Implementation of adaptation actions in health service providing institutions. Adaptation target: By 2030, forty percent (40%) of public sector health service providing institutions will have implemented adaptation actions against possible events associated with climate variability and change. Responsible entities: Ministry of Health and Social Protection.

Strategic allies: National Health Institute (INS). Public sector health service providing institutions. Departmental, district and municipal health secretariats.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Supplementary resources to the health budget for hospital infrastructure adaptations.
  • International funds (PAHO, WHO, IDB, World Bank and others) to strengthen resilience of health services.
  • Co-financing with territorial entities for climate contingency plans in IPS.

2. Capacities:

  • Training of health personnel in response protocols for extreme climate events.
  • Strengthening of risk management teams in public hospitals.
  • Formación en diseño de planes de continuidad operativa.

3. Technology:

  • Integration of IDEAM early warnings with health systems.
  • Digital tools for monitoring climate-related health risks.
  • Innovation in resilient hospital infrastructure (e.g., green roofs, natural ventilation, alternative energy and water systems).

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Inclusion in the Health PIGCCS and territorial health plans.
  • Intersectoral coordination (health, environment, risk management).
  • Participación comunitaria en planes de contingencia sanitaria.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Gestión del Riesgo de Desastres, Infraestructura.

Climate Commitment NDC3A26:

Title: Assessment and analysis of climate change vulnerability in the health sector. Adaptation measure: By 2035, conduct climate vulnerability assessments and analyses in health in at least 30% of the country's departments, using standardized WHO methodologies, integrating results into SIIVRA and the national public health surveillance system, with an intermediate milestone of 15% coverage by 2030. Responsible entities: Ministry of Health and Social Protection.

Strategic allies: National Health Institute (INS). Institute of Hydrology, Meteorology and Environmental Studies (IDEAM). National Planning Department (DNP). Public sector health service providing institutions. Departmental, district and municipal health secretariats. Pan American Health Organization, Andean Health Organization and World Health Organization.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Presupuesto complementario al sector salud para estudios de vulnerabilidad climática.
  • Access to international cooperation (PAHO, WHO, Green Climate Fund).
  • Resources to strengthen resilient health infrastructure and equipment, as well as specialized technical teams and human talent.

2. Capacities:

  • Training of epidemiologists, social and human science professionals, health professionals and technical teams in WHO climate vulnerability methodologies.
  • Strengthening of capacities in departmental, district and municipal health secretariats.
  • Development of capacities for integrating climate and health data for planning and research studies.

3. Technology:

  • Plataformas interoperables entre salud pública y SIIVRA.
  • Incorporation of climate services and early warning systems (SAT) in epidemiological surveillance.
  • Herramientas de modelación para enfermedades sensibles al clima (dengue, malaria, golpe de calor, entre otros).

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Inclusion of results in territorial health plans and in the Health PIGCCS.
  • Interinstitutional coordination between the Ministry of Health and Social Protection, INS, IDEAM and territorial governments.
  • Participación comunitaria y enfoque diferencial (poblaciones rurales, étnicas, niños y adultos mayores).

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Biodiversidad y Servicios Ecosistémicos, Seguridad Alimentaria y.

TRANSPORT SECTOR

Title: Incorporate Green Road Infrastructure Guidelines and the Geotechnical Corridor concept. Adaptation measure: Incorporate the Green Road Infrastructure Guidelines and the Geotechnical Corridor concept in the structuring of new road projects under the transport sector from 2026 onward.

Strategic allies: National Infrastructure Agency (ANI). Transport Infrastructure Planning Unit. National Roads Institute (INVIAS).

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • Specific budget allocation in road infrastructure projects to include green infrastructure components.
  • Access to international funds for pilot tests of climate-resilient geotechnical corridors.
  • Public-private co-financing (road concessionaires) for national scaling.

2. Capacities:

  • Formación en bioingeniería de taludes y soluciones basadas en la naturaleza para ingenieros de transporte.

3. Technology:

  • Geotechnical models integrated with SIIVRA climate data and SSP scenarios from IDEAM.
  • Early warning and monitoring systems in high-slope and high-rainfall corridors. Technological innovations in geotechnics, bioengineering and eco-reduction applied to sustainable and resilient infrastructure development.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Mandatory inclusion of LIVV in sectoral design manuals and bidding documents.
  • Coordination with the Transport PIGCCS and with PIGCCT in regions of high risk from mass movements, flooding, landslides and other hazards.
  • Community participation in the design and monitoring of resilient road corridors.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Hábitat Humano, Gestión del Riesgo de Desastres.

ENVIRONMENT SECTOR

Climate Commitment NDC3A30:

Title: Strengthening of the national monitoring and early warning network by 2030. Adaptation measure: Increase by 2030, from 24% to 35% the monitoring network with real-time transmission connected to early warning systems, and coordinate with the National Unit for Disaster Risk Management the strengthening of territorial capacities for the monitoring, surveillance and permanent evaluation of threats, as well as the timely issuance and dissemination of early warnings. Responsible entities:

Strategic allies: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (OTIC and DCCGR). Other ministries of SISCLIMA. National Planning Department (DNP). Territorial entities.

Support needs:

  • Access to international cooperation (GCF, UNDP, GIZ, IDB, World Bank, among others) for the acquisition and commissioning of automatic hydrometeorological stations.
  • Funds for training and technical support in the territories.

2. Capacities:

  • Training of national and territorial officials in the use and interpretation of hydrometeorological monitoring systems.
  • Institutional strengthening of IDEAM and the Corporations in the maintenance and operation of automatic hydrometeorological stations.
  • Development of community capacities to contribute local information through participatory approaches.

3. Technology:

  • Systems for exchanging hydrometeorological information with regional and local actors.
  • Systems for information access for visualization and analysis by third parties.
  • Digital tools to record hydrometeorological information from other sources or from direct-reading stations.
  • Monitoring and control mechanisms for the automatic station network.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • The information generated in the network is the basis for building indicators for the different monitoring tools.
  • Coordination with the National Disaster Risk Management Plan and regional plans as a monitoring input.
  • Participation of communities, ethnic peoples and civil society in the dissemination of information generated in the network.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: No aplica.

Climate Commitment NDC3A31:

Title: Reduction of areas affected by forest fires by 2030. Adaptation target: By 2030 the country will reduce by 30% the areas affected by forest fires, with respect to 2019, in an articulated and inter-institutional manner, operationalizing the processes for the management, knowledge and reduction of the risk of forest fires and disaster management, through the seven strategies defined in the NDC regarding forest fires. Responsible entities: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DCCGR).11 defined in the NDC regarding forest fires. Responsible entities: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DBBSE y DCCGR)

Strategic allies: National Unit for Disaster Risk Management (UNGRD). National Fire Department of Colombia. Regional Autonomous Corporations (CAR). National Natural Parks of Colombia (PNNC). SINA Institutes – Institute of Hydrology, Meteorology and Environmental Studies (IDEAM), Humboldt Institute and Sinchi Institute.

Governorships and mayor's offices.

Support needs:

1. Climate finance:

  • National and territorial budget resources for forest fire management.
  • International cooperation (FAO, GEF, GCF) for pilot projects and implementation of technological modules for forest fire monitoring, forecasting and detection, research on ecosystem sensitivity to fire, implementation of the national co-responsibility strategy for forest fires, and implementation of the updated national forest fire prevention, control and affected area restoration plan.
  • Cofinanciación con fondos de carbono y PSA (pagos por servicios ambientales) para prevención y restauración post-incendio.

2. Capacities:

  • Technological development and operation: It is essential that entities have trained technical personnel to develop, operate and maintain the monitoring, forecasting, verification and ecosystem modelling modules. Without this specialized knowledge, systems cannot function properly and key decision-making information would not be generated in time.
  • Social co-responsibility: Local communities are the first line for prevention. It is vital to strengthen their capacities through education and training for prevention and early warning. This approach empowers communities, allowing them to take an active role in protecting ecosystems and their services.
  • Institutional strengthening: The updated National Forest Fire Prevention, Control and Affected Area Restoration Plan will include a strategy to improve the technical, operational and administrative capacities of national and territorial entities. This strengthening ensures that entities can plan, coordinate and execute actions effectively, guaranteeing a coordinated response across different levels of government and communities.

3. Technology:

  • The implementation of the first four strategies, corresponding to the technological modules for monitoring, forecasting, fire verification and ecosystem modelling, requires a strong investment in technology and human capital. To achieve these objectives, it is essential to:
  • Contar con personal capacitado y experto: Es fundamental disponer de profesionales con el conocimiento y las habilidades necesarias para desarrollar y operar las herramientas tecnológicas.
  • Access advanced technologies: The acquisition and maintenance of cutting-edge technologies is required for the implementation of these modules.
  • Manage the interoperability of technological modules with other information systems of the national environmental system and the national disaster risk management system.

4. Planning, governance and others:

  • Inclusion of actions for meeting the target and its strategies in territorial risk management plans, territorial environmental management plans, territorial development plans, Territorial and Sectoral PIGCCT, forest management plans and harmonization of instruments.
  • Participation of rural communities and ethnic peoples in implementation of prevention and restoration programs.
  • Support in the implementation of the co-responsibility strategy for forest fire prevention.
  • Support in the implementation of the updated national forest fire prevention, control and affected area restoration plan.

Other SIIVRA dimensions: Riesgo de desastres.

Title: Localities managed under the Master Plan for Coastal Erosion. Adaptation measure: By 2035, coastal erosion points or localities managed under the framework of the updated Master Plan for Coastal Erosion. Responsible entities: Ministry of Environment and Sustainable Development (DAMCRA)

Strategic allies: Institute of Marine and Coastal Research José Benito Vives de Andréis (INVEMAR). National Unit for Disaster Risk Management (UNGRD). General Maritime Directorate (DIMAR). Regional Autonomous Corporations (CAR). Other SINA Institutes. Governorships and mayor's offices.

2. Capacities:

  • Community training in coastal governance and participation in ecosystem-based adaptation (EbA) projects.
